is collision-free and satisfies

\mathbbm{1}_{Qn_{a}Q}=(\mathbbm{1}_{Qn_{t_{1}}Q})^{m_{1}}\circ\ldots\cdot\circ(\mathbbm{1}_{Qn_{t_{n}}Q})^{m_{n}}.

Proof.

Since the elements of
A_{+}:=\left\{n_{a}\,\middle|\,a\in X_{1+}(A_{G})\right\}
each lie in a different double
I
-coset,
\Phi\left(qn_{a}Q\right)=qn_{a}I

defines a bijection
QA_{+}Q/Q\rightarrow IA_{+}I/I
. Therefore, by Proposition
8.2.8
,
\Phi
intertwines the branching operators
\mathbbm{1}_{Qn_{a}Q}

and
\mathbbm{1}_{In_{a}I}
acting on compactly supported functions on
QA_{+}Q/Q
and
IA_{+}I/I
respectively.
Thus, we have

\mathbbm{1}_{Qn_{a}Q}=\Phi^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{In_{a}I}\Phi=\Phi^{-1}\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{In_{t_{i}}I}^{m_{i}}\Phi=\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{Qn_{t_{i}}Q}^{m_{i}},

(on
QA_{+}Q/Q
and thus everywhere by
G
-equivariance of the Hecke action), and it is furthermore collision free since
\mathbbm{1}_{In_{a}I}
is.
∎

The following generalizes Proposition
8.2.1
to deeper Iwahori subgroups.

Corollary 8.2.10
.

Let
Q=I_{r}
be an
r
-deep Iwahori subgroup and
\sigma\geq 2
.
Then there exists a constant
C=C_{Q}>0
, such that for any translation
g=n_{a}\in N_{G}(A_{G})
,
a\in X_{1+}(A_{G})
, and any unitary irreducible representation
\pi
of
G
whose matrix coefficients are in
L^{\sigma+\epsilon}
mod center for all
\epsilon>0
, then

\|\mathbbm{1}_{QgQ}\mid_{\pi^{Q}}\|_{op}\ll(\log X)^{C}X^{\frac{\sigma-1}{\sigma}},\qquad X=|QgQ/Q|,

Proof.

The proof is analogous to Proposition
8.2.1
.
The reduction stated in the first paragraph of the proof of Proposition
8.2.1
follows from the decomposition appearing in
Proposition
8.2.9
and the coset-size comparison Lemma
8.2.6
.

In the second paragraph of the proof of Proposition
8.2.1
, the only place where we used the Iwahori assumption is in the fact that the Iwahori operator is
W
-normal.
We can replace this with input from Bernstein’s uniform admissibility Theorem
[
9
]
, which gives for any open compact subgroup
Q\leq G
a uniform bound
C=N(G,Q)
on the dimension of the subspace of
Q
-fixed vectors of an irreducible representation of
G
. This implies that any operator of the form
\mathbbm{1}_{Qn_{a}Q}
is
C
-normal.

The third paragraph of the proof of Proposition
8.2.1
remains as is, which completes the proof.
∎

We are now in a position to prove our bounds on the Hecke operators
\mathbbm{1}_{PSP}
, where
P
is a flat stabilizer, and
S
is a
P
-stable translation finite set.

Proposition 8.2.11
.

Let
P\leq G
be a flat stabilizer and
S\subset G
a finite
P
-stable translation set such that
|S\cap P|\ll 1
.
For all
\sigma\geq 2
, if a unitary irreducible representation
\pi
of
G
has matrix coefficients in
L^{\sigma+\epsilon}
mod center for all
\epsilon>0
, then

\|\mathbbm{1}_{PSP}\mid_{\pi^{P}}\|_{op}\ll(\log|S|)^{C+d}|S|^{\frac{\sigma-1}{\sigma}},

where
C=C_{Q}>0
, for
Q
a
r
-deep Iwahori contained in
P
, is the constant from Corollary
8.2.10
and
d
is the dimension of the Bruhat-tits building.

Proof.

Without loss of generality,
P
is the stabilizer of a finite set
X\subset\mathcal{A}
, and furthermore, by translating it, let
r
be large enough such that
X\subset\Delta_{r}
, hence
Q_{r}\subset P
.
Then

\|\mathbbm{1}_{PSP}\mid_{\pi^{P}}\|_{op}\leq\sum_{PsP\in P\backslash PSP/P}\|\mathbbm{1}_{PsP}\mid_{\pi^{P}}\|_{op}\leq|P\backslash PSP/P|\max_{PsP\in P\backslash PSP/P}\|\mathbbm{1}_{PsP}\mid_{\pi^{P}}\|_{op},

and combined with Proposition
8.2.5
, Lemma
8.2.6
, and Corollary
8.2.10
, we get

\ll(\log|S|)^{d}\max_{s\in S}\|\mathbbm{1}_{PsP}\mid_{\pi^{P}}\|_{op}\ll(\log|S|)^{d}\max_{s\in S}\|\mathbbm{1}_{QsQ}\mid_{\pi^{Q}}\|_{op}\ll(\log|S|)^{C+d}|S|^{\frac{\sigma-1}{\sigma}},

which completes the proof.
∎

We now apply Proposition
8.2.11
to the settings we previously considered:

Corollary 8.2.12
.

Let
K^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}
be as defined above.
For all
\sigma\geq 2
, if a unitary irreducible representation
\pi
of
G
has matrix coefficients in
L^{\sigma+\epsilon}
mod center for all
\epsilon>0
, then for some constant
C^{\prime}>0
,

\|\mathbbm{1}_{K^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}K^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}}\|_{op}\ll(\log|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|)^{C^{\prime}}|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|^{\frac{\sigma-1}{\sigma}}.

Proof.

Let
K_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq K^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}
be special. Rephrasing proposition
3.4.2
(3),
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}
is a the set of
s\in\Lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}
satisfying a condition depending only on
K_{\mathfrak{p}}sK_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Therefore, since
4.1.3
(1) gives that
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}\to G_{\mathfrak{p}}/K^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is surjective,
K^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}K^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}=K_{\mathfrak{p}}\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}K_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}
is in addition
K_{\mathfrak{p}}
-stable.

In particular, using the class-number-one property once more and that all
K_{\mathfrak{p}}
-double cosets are represented by translations, we can find a
K_{\mathfrak{p}}
-stable translation set
R\subseteq\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}
such that
K_{\mathfrak{p}}\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}K_{\mathfrak{p}}=K_{\mathfrak{p}}RK_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Therefore, this follows from Proposition
8.2.11
for
P=K_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
S=R
combined with Corollary
4.3.9
.
∎

Remark 8.2.13
.

When
\mathbbm{1}_{K^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}K^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is normal, the constant
C^{\prime}
in Corollary
8.2.12
can be take to be the rank
d
of
G_{\mathfrak{p}}
since the constant
C
coarsely bounding the failure of normality in Proposition
8.2.11
can then be taken to be
1
.

Normality is guaranteed when, in addition to being Weyl-complete (recall Definition
3.3.10
), the subset
\Sigma_{0},\Sigma_{0}^{\mathrm{sc}},\Sigma_{\tau}
or
\Sigma_{\tau}^{\mathrm{sc}}
of cocharacters defining the gate set
S_{\Lambda}
as in
4.2.1
/
4.2.4
is
symmetric
—i.e, every element of
-\Sigma_{0}
is Weyl-conjugate to an element of
\Sigma_{0}
. This holds for all the examples in
4.2.6
and therefore all the examples in Table
4.1
.

8.3
Optimal Covering Proof

We can now put everything together to prove Theorem
8.0.1
using the identifications

L^{2}_{\Box}=V_{\Box}:=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\Box\\
\pi_{\infty\setminus v_{0}}\text{ trivial}\end{subarray}}m_{\pi}\pi_{v_{0}}\boxtimes(\pi^{\infty})^{K^{\infty}}.

We start with a corollary of the density hypothesis, which allows us to interpolate an inequality from
2/\sigma=1
and
2/\sigma=0
to all values in between:

Proposition 8.3.1
.

If
n=4,8
, for any shape
\Box
, any
\ell
, and
\epsilon^{N_{G}-1}\lesssim|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|^{-1}
,

\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\mid V_{\Box}\|^{2}_{op}\cdot\|\Proj_{\Box}f_{v_{0}}^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}\|^{2}_{2}\lesssim|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|^{-1}\cdot\|f_{v_{0}}^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}\|^{2}_{2}.

Proof.

This is a reformulation of Corollaries
7.3.2
and
7.3.4
.

We expand all factors in terms of
\epsilon
and the
2/\sigma_{\Box}
from
6.4.4
: Theorem
7.3.1
upper-bounds
\|\Proj_{\Box}f_{v_{0}}^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}\|^{2}_{2}/\|f_{v_{0}}^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}\|^{2}_{2}
. Theorem
6.4.2
and the extra computations in the proof of
7.3.4
lower bounds
2/\sigma(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})
for any
\pi\in\Box
by
2/{\sigma_{\Box}}
. Therefore, we can input
\sigma=\sigma_{\Box}
into Corollary
8.2.12
to upper bound
\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\mid V_{\Box}\|^{2}_{op}
.

This reduces our desired bound to
\epsilon^{A(\sigma_{\Box})}\lesssim\epsilon^{B(\sigma_{\Box})}
, where
A
and
B
are certain linear functions in
2/\sigma_{\Box}
. We can easily check that
A\geq B
when
2/\sigma_{\Box}=0,1
.
∎

While conceptually cleaner, Proposition
8.3.1
will only give a
|S^{(\ell)}|^{\epsilon}
in the numerator of the covering condition
1.2.1
(1). To improve this to a log factor:

Proposition 8.3.2
.

If
n=4,8
, for any shape
\Box
, any
\ell
, and
\epsilon^{N_{G}-1}\ll|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|^{-1}
, there is a
c>0
such that

\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\mid V_{\Box}\|^{2}_{op}\cdot\|\Proj_{\Box}f_{v_{0}}^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}\|^{2}_{2}\ll\left(\log|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|\right)^{c}|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|^{-1}\cdot\|f_{v_{0}}^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}\|^{2}_{2}.

Proof.

The only bound in the proof of
8.3.1
that requires the
\lesssim
instead of
\ll
is that on
\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\mid V_{\Box}\|^{2}_{op}
coming from Corollary
8.2.12
. For
2/\sigma_{\Box}\neq 0,1
, Remark
7.3.3
allows us to tighten the bound
7.3.1
on
\|\Proj_{\Box}f_{v_{0}}^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}\|^{2}_{2}
to nevertheless improve the
\lesssim
to an
\ll
. For
2/\sigma_{\Box}=0
, we can use the trivial bound
\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\|^{2}_{op}\ll 1
. ∎

For convenience, we re-index

I_{\delta}:=f^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}_{v_{0}}

for the
\epsilon
such that it has support of volume
\delta
. In particular
\delta\asymp\epsilon^{N_{G}-1}
so Proposition
8.3.1
applies to
\delta\lesssim|S_{p}^{[\ell]}|^{-1}
.

Note also that the projection of this support onto
PU(n)
has the same volume (normalizing
\vol(U(n))=\vol(PU(n))=1
) and is a ball in an invariant metric. In other words

\supp(\bar{f}^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}_{v_{0}}:PU(n)\to\mathbb{C})=B^{PU(n)}(\delta).

In addition, since
f^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}_{v_{0}}
is analytic, constant on
U(1)
orbits, and equal to
1
at the identity,

\langle I_{\delta},\mathbf{1}\rangle=(\delta+o(\delta)).

(8.2)

Combining the above propositions, we are now in a position to estimating the covering rate of
S_{p}
in terms of the spectrum of the operators
T_{S_{p}^{[\ell]}}
evaluated on each subspace
V_{\Box}
separately.

Proposition 8.3.3
.

If
n=4,8
, the analogue of the optimal covering property Definition
1.2.1
) for
PU(n)
holds for
S_{\mathfrak{p}}\cup\Gamma
when
K^{\prime}
is almost golden at
\mathfrak{p}
(resp. the super version for finite subgroup
C_{\mathfrak{p}}
and finite-order elements
{}^{0}\!S_{\mathfrak{p}}
when
K^{\prime}
is almost
\tau
-super-golden at
\mathfrak{p}
for
\tau
traversable).

In other words, there is a constant
c
such that

\mu\left(PU(n)\setminus B\left(S^{[\ell]_{\mathfrak{p}}},\varepsilon_{\ell}\right)\right)\overset{{\scriptscriptstyle\ell\rightarrow\infty}}{\longrightarrow}0,\qquad\varepsilon_{\ell}=\frac{\left(\log|S^{[\ell]}_{\mathfrak{p}}|\right)^{c}}{|S^{[\ell]}_{\mathfrak{p}}|},

Proof.

Our parameter is
\ell
, and we take
\delta
as a function of
\ell
:

\delta:=\frac{(\log|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|)^{c}}{|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|}\quad,\quad h_{\delta}:=I_{\delta}-\langle I_{\delta},\mathbf{1}\rangle\mathbf{1}.

Denote by
\varphi\mapsto\varphi_{*}
the normalized (i.e, left-inverse to pullback) pushforward map from
U(N)
to
\Gamma\backslash U(N)
. Then for any
\varphi
, we have
T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}(\varphi_{*})=(T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\varphi)_{*}
since the operator descends to
L^{2}(\Gamma\backslash U(n))
.

Therefore we can compute on the one hand,

\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}(h_{\delta,*})\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\int_{U(n)}\left[T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\left(I_{\delta}-\langle I_{\delta},\mathbf{1}\rangle\mathbf{1}\right)(x)\right]^{2}\,dx,

so since the support of
T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\left(I_{\delta}\right)
is contained in the pullback to
U(n)
of
B(S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]},\delta)\subseteq PU(n)
,

\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}(h_{\delta,*})\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{|\Gamma|}\int_{PU(n)\setminus B(S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]},\delta)}\left[\langle I_{\delta},\mathbf{1}\rangle\mathbf{1}(x)\right]^{2}\,dx=(\delta+o(\delta))^{2}\cdot\mu\left(PU(n)\setminus B(S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]},\delta)\right)

using (
8.2
) for the last step.

On the other hand,

\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}h_{\delta,*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}I_{\delta}\|_{2}^{2}=\left\|\sum_{\Box}T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\Proj_{\Box}I_{\delta,*}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{\Box}\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\Proj_{\Box}I_{\delta,*}\|_{2}^{2}\\
\leq\sum_{\Box}\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}\mid V_{\Box}\|_{op}^{2}\cdot\|\Proj_{\Box}I_{\delta,*}\|_{2}^{2},

(8.3)

so noting that the number of possible
\Box
is a constant depending only on
n
, Proposition
8.3.2
gives that for some
c_{0}
:

\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}h_{\delta,*}\|_{2}^{2}\ll\left(\log|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|\right)^{c_{0}}|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|^{-1}\|I_{\delta,*}\|_{2}^{2}\asymp\left(\log|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|\right)^{c_{0}}|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|^{-1}|\Gamma\cap U(1)|^{-1}\delta.

for small
\delta
.

Without loss of generality,
c_{0}<c
. Then, combining the two estimates together, we get

\mu\left(PU(n)\setminus B(S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]},\delta)\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{(\log|S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}|)^{c-c_{0}}}\xrightarrow{\ell\rightarrow\infty}0,

which proves that
S_{\mathfrak{p}}
has the optimal covering property.
∎

Finally, we note that Theorem
8.0.1
follows from
Theorem
4.4.1
and Proposition
8.3.3
. We make some further remarks:

Remark 8.3.4
.

If we set

\epsilon_{\ell}=\frac{(\log|S^{[\ell]}|)^{c}}{|S^{[2\ell]}|}\asymp\frac{(\log|S^{[\ell]}|)^{c}}{|S^{[\ell]}|^{2}},

then the arguments of
[
29
]
*Prop 4.6/
[
70
]
*Cor 3.2 show that for large enough
\ell
,

\mu\left(PU(n)\setminus B\left(S^{[\ell]},\varepsilon_{\ell}\right)\right)=0.

In other words, even the small volume of exceptional points that cannot be approximated by words of the “optimal” length
\ell
can be approximated by words of length
2\ell
.

Remark 8.3.5
.

When
K^{\prime}
does not have class number one, lemma
8.1.2
no longer interprets
T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}
as a Hecke operator since we no longer necessarily have that
KS_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}K=S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}K
. However, we may instead replace
T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}
by the Clozel-Hecke operator of
[
29
, Def 4.3]
that takes a weighted sum over a set of Clozel-Hecke points also defined therein.

As in the proof of
[
29
, Thm 4.4]
, this Clozel-Hecke operator has operator norm bounded by that of a Hecke operator. This Hecke operator’s norm can further be bounded in terms of the total weight of the Clozel-Hecke points exactly as in Proposition
8.3.2
. The proof of Proposition
8.3.3
then carries through in exactly the same way to prove optimal covering for the set of Clozel-Hecke points.

Remark 8.3.6
.

One might expect that the corresponding
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}
could give golden gate sets for
U(n)
by using the argument for
8.3.3
with respect to
f_{v_{0}}^{\epsilon,Z_{\epsilon}}
instead of
f_{v_{0}}^{\epsilon,Z_{1}}
.

However, there is an obstruction that the determinant is constant on
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}
as defined here. In the argument, this shows up in that Theorem
7.3.1
needs to be tightened to a bound instead by
\epsilon^{N_{G}(1-2/\sigma_{\Box})}
, which turns out to hold for all
\Box
except the trivial shape
(n,1)
corresponding to automorphic characters. Therefore, in the bound (
8.3
), the term for
\Box=(n,1)
has to be handled separately by noting that
h_{\delta}
has no component along the trivial character and bounding
\|T_{S_{\mathfrak{p}}^{[\ell]}}|\chi\|_{op}
for non-trivial characters
\chi
.

This operator norm bound can be done by Weyl equidistribution-type estimates, but only if
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}
has elements with determinants differing by a non-root of unity. Therefore, modifying
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{p}}
by multiplying its elements by differing points in
U(1)
should suffice to produce a gate set on
U(n)
.
